# Args:
# 1 - instance name
dump_postgresql () {
    local INSTANCE_NAME=$1
    local TIMESTAMP=$( date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S )

    echo "Dumping database..."
    local DUMP_FILE=${schema_name}_dump_$TIMESTAMP.tar.gz
    docker exec -i ${INSTANCE_NAME} /bin/bash -c "PGPASSWORD=${schema_name} pg_dump --username=${schema_name} -F c ${schema_name}" > $DUMP_FILE || exit
    echo "Database dumped to $DUMP_FILE"
}

upgrade_postgresql_schema () {
    mvnd judo-rdbms-schema:apply \
    ${mavenVersionArg} \
    -DjdbcUrl=jdbc:postgresql://127.0.0.1:${POSTGRES_PORT:-5432}/${schema_name} \
    -DdbType=postgresql \
    -DdbUser=${schema_name} \
    -DdbPassword=${schema_name} \
    -DschemaIgnoreModelDependency=true \
    -DupdateModel=${SCHEMA_DIR}/model/target/generated-resources/model/${schema_name}-rdbms_postgresql.model \
    -f ${SCHEMA_DIR}/schema || exit
}

install_maven_wrapper () {
    mvn wrapper:wrapper -Dmaven=3.8.6 || exit
    sed $(get_sed_edit_option) 's/https:\/\/nexus\.judo\.technology\/repository\/maven-judong\//https:\/\/repo\.maven\.apache\.org\/maven2\//; s/https\:\/\//####/; s/\/\//\//; s/####/https\:\/\//' ${MODEL_DIR}/.mvn/wrapper/maven-wrapper.properties
        exit 1
  fi
  source ${MODEL_DIR}/${profile}.properties
elif [ -f "${MODEL_DIR}/judo.properties" ]; then
	sour

[3759] TOOLRESULT: 
`./judo.sh COMMANDS... [OPTIONS...]` - several commands can be chained (`./judo.sh build clean start`).

| Command | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `env <env>` | Use an alternate custom properties file (default `judo.properties`), e.g. `./judo.sh env compose-dev build start` |
| `clean` | Stop the PostgreSQL container and clear its data |
| `prune` (`-f`, `-y`) | Delete untracked files (see warning below) |
| `update` (`-i`) | Update dependency versions in the JUDO project |
| `generate` (`-i`) | Regenerate the application from the model (`-DgenerateApplication -DskipApplicationBuild`) |
| `generate-root` (`-i`) | Regenerate the application root structure (`-DgenerateRoot`) |
| `dump` | Dump PostgreSQL data before clearing and starting |
| `import` (`-dn <name>`) | Import a dump; without a name the last one is loaded |
| `schema-upgrade` | Persistent PostgreSQL only: generates the difference against the running database and applies it |
| `build` | Build the project (options below) |
| `reckless` | Build and run as fast as possible (skips validation and Docker) |
| `start` | Run the application with PostgreSQL and Keycloak |
| `stop` | Stop application, PostgreSQL and Keycloak |
| `status` | Print the status of the containers |

**`build` options:** `-v <version>` (model and application version), `-p` (parallel), `-a` (app module only), `-f` (frontend module only), `-sc` (build the schema CLI standalone JAR), `-d` / `--docker` (build Docker images), `-M` (skip model), `-B

## Intent
When a model change adds an attribute, the generated schema migration adds a plain nullable column and leaves every existing row at NULL. That ignores a required flag or a default expression on the attribute, so old rows read as "unset" while new rows get the default. A small hand-written changeset closes the gap by filling existing rows once, in the same migration step that adds the column.

[3774] TOOLRESULT: ## Quick Start Workflow

This section provides a condensed workflow for the most common migration task: generating and applying a schema update in a local development environment.

1.  **Ensure your model changes are built:**
    ```bash
    # From the project root
    cd application/model
    mvn clean install
    ```

2.  **Generate the migration scripts:**
    The `generate` goal takes the base and the updated RDBMS model files (`baseModel`, `updateModel`; each a file path or a `mvn:` URL such as `mvn:<groupId>:<app>-model:<version>!model/<app>-rdbms_postgresql.model`), the `modelName`, the `dbType` and an `outputDirectory`. If no base model is given, the base is read from the `latest.version` / `previous.version` marker files in the output directory.
    ```bash
    # From application/schema/
    mvn judo-rdbms-schema:generate \
      -DmodelName=<app> -DdbType=postgresql \
      -DbaseModel=<base_model_file_or_mvn_url> \
      -DupdateModel=<update_model_file_or_mvn_url>
    ```
    The `execute` goal takes `baseModelVersion` and `updateModelVersion` (see below).

3.  **Apply the migration to your local database:**
    ```bash
    # From application/schema/
    mvn judo-rdbms-schema:execute \
      -DbaseModelVersion=<base_version> \
      -DupdateModelVersion=<update_version> \
      -DjdbcUrl=jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5432/<app> \
      -DdbUser=<app> \
      -DdbPassword=[password]
    ```

4.  **Start the application** to verify the changes:
    ```bash
    # From 
[3775] TOOLRESULT: ## Schema Migration Workflow

### Step 1: Identify Model Versions

When migrating, you need two model versions:
- **Base version** (FROM): Current database schema version
- **Update version** (TO): Target database schema version

**Model versions** are embedded in the generated model artifacts. The generated model POM sets `model-version` to `${project.version}`; build pipelines often use a timestamped form such as `1.1.0.20210929_004050_9602796_develop` (set it with `./judo.sh build -v <version>`).

**Important**: This is the MODEL version (the `Judo-Models` bundle header / `model-version` property), NOT a separate application version.

### Step 2: Generate Migration Scripts

**Automatic generation** compares the base and the updated RDBMS model:

```bash
cd application/schema

mvn judo-rdbms-schema:generate \
  -DmodelName=<app> -DdbType=postgresql \
  -DbaseModel=<base rdbms model file or mvn: url> \
  -DupdateModel=<update rdbms model file or mvn: url> \
  -DoutputDirectory=migration
```

**What gets generated** in `migration/<previous>_to_<latest>/` (dots and dashes of the versions become underscores):
- an aggregated `00_<app>_incremental_<dialect>.changeset.xml` plus eight phase changesets: `check`, `backup`, `beforeIncremental`, `updateDataBeforeIncremental`, `incremental`, `updateDataAfterIncremental`, `aferIncremental` (sic, script `07_aferIncremental`) and `dropBackup`;
- the model `<app>-rdbms_<version>_<dialect>.model`;
- marker files `latest.version` and `previo

// One price format for the whole shop: two decimals plus currency. The number follows the UI language (F40):
// "79.90 EUR" in English, "79,90 EUR" in Hungarian.
export const PRICE_CURRENCY = 'EUR';

export function formatPrice(value?: number | string | null): string {
  if (value === null || value === undefined || value === '') {
    return '';
  }
  const n = typeof value === 'number' ? value : Number(value);
  if (Number.isNaN(n)) {
    return '';
  }
  const language = i18n.language || 'en-US';
  const number = new Intl.NumberFormat(language, { minimumFractionDigits: 2, maximumFractionDigits: 2 }).format(n);
  return `${number} ${PRICE_CURRENCY}`;
}

// Read-only display (used by cards and any custom page).
export const Price: FC<{ value?: number | null }> = ({ value }) => {
  useTranslation(); // re-render when the language changes
  return <span data-testid="price">{formatPrice(value)}</span>;
};

  data: { price?: number | null; __updateable?: boolean };
  validation: Map<string, string>;
  editMode?: boolean;
  isLoading?: boolean;
  storeDiff: (attributeName: any, value: any) => void;
  required?: boolean;
};

[3795] TOOLRESULT: import { useEffect } from 'react';
import { useConfig } from '~/hooks';
import { useLayoutHelper } from '~/utilities/layout-helper';

// The generated layout stores "mini drawer" in the browser and only changes it when the window crosses a size
// tier. A window that was once small therefore opens collapsed (icons only, truncated labels, tiny logo) on a
// desktop screen. This component has no visuals: on laptop and wide screens it opens the drawer when the page
// loads and whenever the size tier changes. Registered as the documented AppBar extra component
// (agent-docs/frontend/hooks/ui-hooks.md); see GAPS.md R8-1.
export const DrawerExpander = () => {
  const { onChangeMiniDrawer } = useConfig();
  const { size } = useLayoutHelper();
  useEffect(() => {
    if (size === 'lg' || size === 'xl') {
      onChangeMiniDrawer(false);
    }
  }, [size]);
  return null;
};

## Intent
Deep entity hierarchies (for example galaxy, star, planet) make the browser back button an unreliable way to go up one level. A navigation hook that records a breadcrumb stack gives consistent back behavior and a visible trail.
## Practice
### Platform base
- The generated React frontend exports `useJudoNavigation()` from `~/components`. It returns `{ navigate, clearNavigate, externalNavigate, navigateBackTo, back, isBackDisabled, setTitle }`; `navigate` takes a path string or `{ pathname, search }` and applies the registered navigation interceptor ([type-safe navigation](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/navigati

2010:### Table Column Customization
### Table Column Customization
-   **Overview:** Replaces the default rendering of a specific table column with a custom component, ideal for displaying icons, badges, or complex data.
-   **Implementation:** A `ColumnCustomizerHook` is registered, targeted at a specific `component` (table name) and `column`. The hook returns a new column definition, typically overriding the `renderCell` function.
---

[3823] TOOLRESULT: ## Custom Columns

### Purpose

Add custom columns to tables with custom rendering logic. Useful for computed values, status indicators, or action buttons.

### Basic Implementation

```typescript
// src/custom/hooks/columnCustomizer.tsx
import type { ColumnCustomizerHook } from '~/utilities';
import { GridRenderCellParams } from '@mui/x-data-grid';
import { Chip } from '@mui/material';
import type { ServiceLocationStored } from '~/services/data-api/model/ServiceLocation';

export const locationStatusColumn: ColumnCustomizerHook<ServiceLocationStored> = (original) => ({
  ...original,
  field: 'qualityScore',
  headerName: 'Validation Status',
  width: 180,
  renderCell: (params: GridRenderCellParams) => {
    const quality = params.row.qualityScore;
    const colorMap = {
      'HIGH': 'success',
      'MEDIUM': 'warning',
      'LOW': 'info',
      'NONE': 'error'
    };

    return (
      <Chip
        label={quality}
        color={colorMap[quality] || 'default'}
        size="small"
      />
    );
  },
});
```

### Registration

```typescript
// src/custom/application-customizer.tsx
import {
  TABLE_COLUMN_CUSTOMIZER_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY,
  type ColumnCustomizerHook,
} from '~/utilities';
import { locationStatusColumn } from './hooks/columnCustomizer';

context.registerService<ColumnCustomizerHook<ServiceLocationStored>>(
  TABLE_COLUMN_CUSTOMIZER_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY,
  locationStatusColumn,
  { component: 'ServiceLocationsTable', column: 'qualityScore' }
);
```

title: Audit Log Entity
description: "An append-only AuditLog entity that records who did what to which entity and when, with the actor name copied into each record so it stays readable after the user changes."

## Intent

Keep an immutable record of significant changes so that administrators can see who did what, to which entity, and when. Rows are written by backend code only. No user creates, edits or deletes them through the generated actions.

### Audit log is append-only

The entity turns off generated create, update and delete. Rows are written by operations that record their own action.

### Actor name is copied into the record

The user name and organization name are stored as plain attributes, so a record stays readable after the user or organization is renamed or removed. The association to the user is kept for navigation onl
[3882] CALL ctx_execute: {"language": "shell", "code": "cd ~/Projects/judo-blueprint/catalog; cat model-blueprints/audit-log-entity/backend.md model-blueprints/audit-log-entity/model.md | cut -c1-700; grep -n -i \"update\\|before\\|old\" agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md | head -50 | cut -c1-220"}
[3883] TOOLRESULT: ## Backend guidance

### Write audit rows from custom operations

Interceptors run only for built-in operations (create, update, delete, list and similar). Custom operations and script operations get no interceptor around them. So an audit row for a custom operation has to be written by that operation. Put the call last, after the business action, in the same operation call.

- Keep one shared audit service (project code) with two methods: one that resolves the current user and organization from the actor context, and one for automated actions that have no user. The platform offers a variable resolver for the actor context; the service itself is yours.
- Each custom operation calls the service once with the action type, entity type, entity id and a details map.
- Serialize the details map to JSON in the service, not in each operation.
- The service looks up the organization name and writes it with the user name.

### Keep audit failures from breaking the business action

Wrap the audit write in a try/catch that logs the error and returns. A failure to write the audit row must not roll back a business change. See [[safe-failure-pattern]].

### Built-in operations: audit with an interceptor

If every built-in create, update and delete must be audited, a post-call interceptor can write the row. This is a variation for built-in operations only: the interceptor never runs for custom operations, so keep the explicit calls too. Synchronous interceptors run inside the operation's tra

[3886] TOOLRESULT: ## Example 3: Update Interceptor with Batch Processing

**Pattern**: Intercept update and process multiple related entities

```java
@Component(property = { "judo.model.name=<app>" })
public class AccessRoleUpdateInterceptor implements OperationCallInterceptor {

    @Reference EntityDao entityDao;
    @Reference RecalculateRelatedData recalculateRelatedData;

    private AsmUtils asmUtils;
    private List<EOperation> interceptedOperations;

    @Override
    public String getName() {
        return this.getClass().getSimpleName();
    }

    @Override
    public Collection<EOperation> getOperations(AsmModel asmModel) {
        if (interceptedOperations == null) {
            if (asmUtils == null) {
                asmUtils = new AsmUtils(asmModel.getResourceSet());

            ).map(asmUtils::resolveOperation).map(Optional::orElseThrow).toList();
        }
        return interceptedOperations;
    }

[3889] TOOLRESULT: | `preCall` | Runs before the decorated call. The returned object replaces the input payload and must be assignable to the expected payload type (otherwise `IllegalArgumentException`). When `ignoreDecoratedCall()` is `true` it must return the payload of the decorated call's return type. |
| `postCall` | Runs after the decorated call with the input and the call's return payload (a `Payload` or a collection of payloads). The returned object becomes the result. |
| `ignoreDecoratedCall()` | `true` skips the platform's own DAO call, so the interceptor replaces the operation. The decorated call runs only if no interceptor for the operation returns `true`. |
| `async()` | `true` runs the interceptor on a different thread, outside the transaction and without context variables. Its exceptions are lost and it cannot change the payload. |
| `terminateOnException()` | Defaults to `true`: an exception terminates the call. Ignored for async interceptors. |

Interceptors may throw only `InterceptorCallBusinessException` (see [Throwing a Business Error from an Interceptor](#throwing-a-business-error-from-an-interceptor)).

### Which Operations Are Intercepted

Interceptors fire **only for the built-in behaviour operations**: create, update, delete, list/query, refresh, set/unset/add/remove reference, reference range, input range, getTemplate, getMetadata, getPrincipal, upload token, export and the validate calls. These are the operations that the runtime implements itself (the `*Call` class

[3907] TOOLRESULT: ## Running Tests

### Run All Integration Tests

```bash
mvn clean test -pl application/integration-test
```

### Run Specific Test Class

```bash
mvn clean test -pl application/integration-test -Dtest=UserDaoTest
```

### Run Specific Test Method

```bash
mvn clean test -pl application/integration-test -Dtest=UserDaoTest#testCreateUser
```

### Run with Debug Logging

Edit `src/test/resources/logback-test.xml`:

```xml
<configuration>
    <appender name="STDOUT" class="ch.qos.logback.core.ConsoleAppender">
        <encoder>
            <pattern>%d{HH:mm:ss.SSS} [%thread] %-5level %logger{36} - %msg%n</pattern>
        </encoder>
    </appender>

    <root level="DEBUG">
        <appender-ref ref="STDOUT" />
    </root>
</configuration>
```

Then run:

```bash
mvn clean test -pl application/integration-test
```

### Skip Tests During Build

```bash
mvn clean install -DskipTests
```

[3921] TOOLRESULT: | `implies` | Logical material implication |

### Enumeration
Represents a value from a predefined set of members. Members are referenced by `EnumerationName#MEMBER_NAME`.

| Operation | Result | Meaning |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `==`, `!=` | Boolean | Equality comparison. |
| `!asString()`| String | Returns the literal name of the member as a string. |

### Date & Timestamp
-   **Date**: Represents a calendar date. Literals are delimited by backticks (e.g., `` `2024-01-01` ``).
-   **Timestamp**: Represents a point in time with a mandatory time zone. Literals are ISO-8601 strings in backticks (e.g., `` `2024-01-01T12:00:00Z` ``).

Both support standard chronological comparisons (`<`, `>`, `==`, etc.) and `!asString()`.

#### Temporal functions

| Function | Applies to | Meaning |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `<Type>!now()` | Date, Timestamp, Time types | Current date / timestamp / time. These are `SYSTEM`-category environment variables (`current_date`, `current_timestamp`, `current_time`), e.g. `<app>::types::Timestamp!now()`. As an attribute default it fills the value at creation. |
| `!year()`, `!month()`, `!day()` | Date, Timestamp | Extract the part as an integer. |
| `!hour()`, `!minute()`, `!second()`, `!millisecond()` | Timestamp, Time | Extract the part. |
| `!dayOfWeek()`, `!dayOfYear()` | Date | Day number within the week / year. |
| `!date()`, `!time()` | Timestamp | The date / time part of a timestamp. |
| `<Type>!of(...)` | Constructors | Date: exactly three intege

Every JSL model implicitly imports two framework models. `judo::types` declares `Boolean`, `Date`, `Time`, `Timestamp`, `Integer` (numeric, precision 9, scale 0), `Long` (precision 15, scale 0) (project type libraries often declare their own `Long` with precision 18; the SDK maps precision 10-19 to `java.lang.Long` either way, see [type.md](esm_metamodel/type.md)) and `String` (min-size 0, max-size 4000). `judo::functions` provides the string functions (`matches`, `like`, `ilike`, `replace`, `lpad`, `rpad`, `substring`), `isDefined`/`isUndefined`/`orElse`/`asString` on each type, the collection lambdas (`first`, `last`, `front`, `back`, `filter`, `anyTrue`, `allTrue`, `anyFalse`, `allFalse`, `min`, `max`, `avg`, `sum`), the entity functions (`typeOf`, `kindOf`, `container`, `asType`, `memberOf`, `all`, `any`, `contains`, `size`) and `getVariable(category, key)`, which is only declared on

When JSL is translated to JQL some functions are renamed: `right` to `last`, `left` to `first`, `lower`/`upper` to `lowerCase`/`upperCase`, `size` to `length` (string) or `count` (collection); `first`/`last`/`front`/`back` lambdas become `!heads(...)`/`!tails(...)` with a trailing `DESC`, and `^` becomes repeated multiplication.

[3963] TOOLRESULT: ---
## Intent

Tables with several row-level actions need one consistent action column whose density can be tuned per table: all buttons, one button plus a dropdown, or everything in a dropdown. This practice wraps that in one helper that turns a list of action definitions into a DataGrid column.

## Practice

### Platform base

- The generated React table is an MUI DataGrid. Row-level actions come from the model: row action definitions such as view, remove and delete (`*RowViewButton`, `*RowRemoveButton`, `*RowDeleteButton`) and custom operations placed in a table's `rowOperations` (`OperationFormTableRowCallOperationButton`). See [row actions](../../agent-docs/frontend/model-screen-layout.md) and [table operations](../../agent-docs/frontend/esm-to-ui-mappings/tables-navigation.md).
- The platform has a supported way to add or replace a single column on a generated table without replacing the table: a column customizer hook registered under `TABLE_COLUMN_CUSTOMIZER_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY` with the table component name and the column as service properties. Its documented example is an action button column with `renderCell` ([table hooks](../../agent-docs/frontend/hooks/table-hooks.md)).
- Hooks and custom code you add under `src/custom/` are not generated, so they survive regeneration ([extension points](../../agent-docs/frontend/extension-points.md)).

### Project practice: configurable action column helper

/**
 * F36: the one clock of the custom Java code (best-practices/backend/injectable-business-clock.md).
 * Production: the system clock. A demo or test run can start the application with the environment variable
 * WEBSHOP_BUSINESS_DATE (ISO date or date-time) or the system property of the same name: the clock then starts at that
 * moment and keeps advancing. It is read once at class load, so changing it needs a restart: a browser test cannot set it
 * (GAPS R10). Model expressions (`Date!now()`, F37 sale window) use the platform clock, not this one.
 */
public final class BusinessClock {
    private static final Logger LOG = LoggerFactory.getLogger(BusinessClock.class);
    private static final Clock CLOCK = create();

    private BusinessClock() {}

    private static Clock create() {
        Clock system = Clock.systemDefaultZone();
        String raw = System.getProperty("WEBSHOP_BUSINESS_DATE", System.getenv("WEBSHOP_BUSINESS_DATE"));
        if (raw == null || raw.isBlank()) {
            return system;
        }
        try {
            LocalDateTime fake = raw.contains("T") ? LocalDateTime.parse(raw.trim()) : LocalDate.parse(raw.trim()).atStartOfDay();
            Duration offset = Duration.between(Instant.now(), fake.atZ

## Intent

Part of a page needs UI the generator cannot produce (charts, masked inputs, rich viewers). Model that part as a custom implementation element and supply the React component yourself; the rest of the page stays generated.

## Practice

### Platform base

- A custom implementation is a visual element of the ESM UI model that the generator exposes as an extension point. It is not a way to swap an arbitrary generated component: the element must be modeled first ([widgets: placeholder / custom implementation](../../agent-docs/frontend/esm-to-ui-mappings/widgets.md), [advanced patterns: custom component replacement](../../agent-docs/frontend/advanced-patterns.md)).
- You register a function component as a Pandino service under `CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY` (exported from `~/custom`) with the service property `component` set to the per-element key generated in the container's `customization.ts`. There is no generic `CUSTOM_COMPONENT_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY`.
- For containers that have such elements the generator creates the scaffold `src/custom/hooks/custom-implementations/register<Container>CustomImplementations.tsx.default` with one `register<Interface>CustomImplementation(context)` function per element and the right props type (`GenericProxyProps`, `TableProxyProps`, `LinkProxyProps` from `~/custom`). Copy it without `.default`, implement the component and call the register function from `application-customizer.tsx` ([hooks: using .default scaffolds](../../agen

   * From: relation.isListable, relation.isCollection
   * @throws {AxiosError} With data containing {@link Array<FeedbackItem>} for status codes: 401, 403.

  async list(
    owner?: JudoIdentifiable<any>,
    queryCustomizer?: WishlistItemInfoQueryCustomizer,
    headers?: Record<string, string>,
  ): Promise<JudoRestResponse<Array<WishlistItemInfoStored>>> {

}

// Details page: the generated "Add to cart" action asks for a quantity.
export async function addFromDetails(page: Page, product: string, quantity: number) {
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, card(page, product), /.*~get$/);
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: /Add to cart/ }).click();
  const d = page.getByRole('dialog');
  await expect(d).toBeVisible();
  const box = d.getByRole('textbox', { name: /Quantity/ }).or(d.getByRole('spinbutton', { name: /Quantity/ }));
  await box.click();
  await box.fill(String(quantity));
  await box.blur();
  await page.waitForTimeout(700);
  await d.getByRole('button', { name: /(Ok|Submit)$/ }).click();
}

export async function openCart(page: Page) {
  await goToMenu(page, 'Cart');
  await expect(page.getByTestId('cart').or(page.getByTestId('cart-empty'))).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
}

// The shop home (card grid) is the start page.
export async function backToShop(page: Page) {
  await apiHelper.gotoWithWait(page);
  await expect(page.getByTestId('catalog-grid')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 30000 });
}

}

The generated `playwright.config.ts` builds this path from the model name and the application (actor) name. Example: `http://localhost:8181/<app>/Actor`. Check the `baseURL` in your generated `playwright.config.ts` for the exact value.

## Authentication (Actors with Principal)

When an Actor has a **principal** (authenticated user), the application requires login through Keycloak. Configure Playwright to handle authentication:

1. Create `tests/auth.setup.ts` to perform Keycloak login
2. Configure `playwright.config.ts` with setup project and `storageState`
3. Tests automatically reuse the saved session

See **[Language-Independent Testing](language-independent-testing.md)** for complete authentication setup examples.

**Note**: Anonymous actors (no principal) don't require authentication setup.

## Running Tests

### Install Dependencies

```bash
cd application/e2e/\<app>__[actor_fqn]/playwright
pnpm install
```

### Run Tests

```bash
# Run all tests (headless)
pnpm test
# or
npx playwright test

# Run tests with UI (interactive mode)
pnpm test:dev
# or
npx playwright test --ui

# Run specific test file
npx playwright test tests/MyTest.spec.ts

# Run with browser visible
npx playwright test --headed

# Debug mode
npx playwright test --debug
```

### View Test Reports

```bash
npx playwright show-report
```

### Video Recording and Screenshots

Playwright can record videos and capture screenshots for debugging failed tests.

**Configuration in `playwright.config.ts`:**

// The back office: actor Admin (F14). Users: admin/admin (scripts/seed-dev-user.sh).
export async function login(page: Page, user = 'admin') {
  await apiHelper.gotoWithWait(page); // app start URL, redirects to Keycloak
  await expect(page.locator('#kc-form-login')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  await page.locator('#username').fill(user);
  await page.locator('#password').fill(user);
  await page.locator('#kc-login').click();
  await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Products' }).first()).toBeVisible({ timeout: 30000 }); // back office menu
}

export async function openProductList(page: Page) {
  await login(page);
  await navigateToAccess(page, 'Products');
  await expect(tableHelper.getRows(page).first()).toBeVisible();
}

// The generated drawer animates its width after a resize; take the screenshot only when the width stopped changing.
export async function settleLayout(page: Page) {
  const width = page.viewportSize()?.width ?? 0;
  if (width >= 1200) {
    // laptop and wide screens: the drawer is open once the logo has its full width
    await expect
      .poll(async () => (await page.locator('img[src*="trail-shop-logo"]').first().boundingBox().catch(() => null))?.width ?? 0, { timeout: 8000 })
      .toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(120)
      .catch(() => undefined);
  }
  await page.waitForTimeout(500);
}

export async function openProductRow(page: Page, name: string) {
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, page.getByRole('gridcell', { name, exact: true }), /.*~get$/);
  await expect(page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Name/ })).toHaveValue(name);
}

// Type into a generated text field with real key presses (see GAPS.md R6-6) and leave it.
export async function typeInto(page: Page, scope: Page | ReturnType<Page['locator']>, label: RegExp, value: string) {
  const box = scope.getByRole('textbox', { name: label });
  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 3; attempt++) {
    await box.click();
    await box.fill('');
    await box.pressSequentially(value, { delay: 10 });
    await box.blur();
    await page.waitForTimeout(700); // DebouncedTextField
    if ((await box.inputValue()) === value) return; // the generated field can drop keys while it re-renders: type again
  }
  await expect(box).toHaveValue(value);
}

export async function saveView(page: Page) {
  await Promise.all([
    page.waitForResponse((r) => r.url().includes('/api/webshop/Admin') && r.request().method() !== 'GET'),
    page.getByRole('button', { name: /Update/ }).click(),
  ]);
}

[4159] TOOLRESULT: ---
title: Runtime Fixture Internals and Test Scoping
description: "This page explains how the testkit builds the runtime for a test, and how to keep integration tests small and fast."
---
# Runtime Fixture Internals and Test Scoping

This page explains how the testkit builds the runtime for a test, and how to keep integration tests small and fast.

## Guice Injector Composition in Tests

`JudoRuntimeFixture` builds the injector in `init(Module module, Object injectModulesTo)`:

```java
Module modules = Modules.combine(module, judoDefaultModule, databaseModule);
injector = Guice.createInjector(modules);
```

- `module` is your test module (bindings for DAOs, mocks, `@JudoTest(modules = …)` classes).
- `judoDefaultModule` is the JUDO runtime (dispatcher, query factory, interceptor provider, coercer).
- `databaseModule` provides the datasource and transaction manager.
- `injectModulesTo` is the test instance; the fixture injects `@Inject` fields into it from the combined modules.

With `@JudoTest` all of this happens in the extension. A custom fixture only needs to call `prepare(modelName, dataSource, dialect)` and then `init(module, testInstance)`. The `getInjector()` result is the source for DAOs, `Dispatcher` and any bound service: `fixture.getInjector().getInstance(UserDao.class)`.

## Fixture Lifecycle per Test

For every test method the extension prepares a fixture, begins a transaction (`AUTO_ROLLBACK` and `AUTO_COMMIT` only), and in the after-each step rolls back (`AUTO
[4160] CALL read: {"path": "/Users/bmiller/Projects/judo-blueprint/catalog/agent-docs/integration-testing/getting-started.md", "offset": 200, "limit": 240}
[4161] TOOLRESULT: 
**Behavior**:
- Transaction starts before test method
- Transaction rolls back after test method (success or failure)
- Database remains clean between tests

**Use when**:
- You want test isolation
- You don't need to verify post-commit behavior
- Most common mode for unit-style integration tests

#### 2. `AUTO_COMMIT`

```java
@JudoTest(transaction = TransactionHandling.AUTO_COMMIT)
public class MyTest {
    // Transactions automatically commit after each test
}
```

**Behavior**:
- Transaction starts before test method
- Transaction commits after successful test
- Transaction rolls back only on test failure

**Use when**:
- You need to verify data persistence
- Testing cascading operations
- Verifying post-commit triggers

**Warning**: Committed data stays until it is truncated; keep `truncateTables = true` (default) or clean up manually

#### 3. `MANUAL`

```java
@JudoTest(transaction = TransactionHandling.MANUAL)
public class MyTest {
    @Test
    public void testWithManualControl(JudoRuntimeFixture fixture) {
        fixture.beginTransaction();
        try {
            // Your test logic
            fixture.commitTransaction();
        } catch (Exception e) {
            fixture.rollbackTransaction();
            throw e;
        }
    }
}
```

**Behavior**:
- No automatic transaction management
- You control `beginTransaction()`, `commitTransaction()`, and `rollbackTransaction()` on the `JudoRuntimeFixture`

**Use when**:
- Testing complex multi-transaction scenarios

[4183] TOOLRESULT: The `application/integration-test` module already declares `…-application-app` as an upstream dependency (see `integration-test/pom.xml`). Because Maven builds `app` → `integration-test` in reactor order, the jar — and therefore every `OSGI-INF/*.xml` resource — is on the test classpath by the time `integration-test`'s `test` phase runs. A plain classpath lookup (`ClassLoader.getResourceAsStream("OSGI-INF/…xml")`) is all that is needed.

> **Orphaned-from-reactor build.** The `application/integration-test` module is intentionally not listed in `application/pom.xml`'s module tree; it is built standalone via `mvn -pl application/integration-test …` (or from the module directory). This is the established JUDO pattern (see <app>'s `application/test/` for a richer example) and is what lets the tests consume the already-packaged upstream app jar without creating a reactor cycle with the `package`-phase bundle plugin.

This blueprint has no model shape: it is a test module. This page describes it: module layout, Maven tiers and tooling.

This layer covers the **structural** side of the testkit foundation: how the test module is organised, how Maven separates the four tiers (DS / Layer-0 / Layer-1 / Layer-2 / pure-infra), the `pom.xml` shape that wires `surefire`, `failsafe`, `-Pdocker`, and the tooling around it (`logback-test.xml`, `.sdkmanrc`, dependency pinning).

    protected FaultRegistry createTestFaultRegistry(JudoRuntimeFixture fixture, 
                                                   Address facility, 
                                                   User assignedTo, 
                                                   String registryNumber) {
        FaultRegistryDao faultRegistryDao = fixture.getInjector().getInstance(FaultRegistryDao.class);
        return faultRegistryDao.create(FaultRegistryForCreate.builder()
                .withRegistryNumber(registryNumber)
                .withFacility(facility)
                .withAssignedTo(assignedTo)
                .withOfferCreated(false)
                .build());
    }

[4316] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md", "section": "Backend Platform Behavior Reference > Time and Time Zones"}
[4317] TOOLRESULT: 
Two clocks exist and they do not agree:

- `Timestamp!now()` / `current_timestamp` in model expressions follows the platform parameter `defaultZoneId` (`JUDO_PLATFORM_DEFAULT_ZONE_ID`; the configuration template defaults to `UTC`), not the JVM time zone.
- `LocalDateTime.now()` in custom Java follows the JVM `TZ` / `user.timezone`.
- The persistence mapper converts to UTC and the JSON layer serializes ISO offset/UTC values.

Writing with one clock and comparing against the other yields a fixed offset (for example two hours in a Budapest summer) that silently breaks age or duration limits. Use one clock for both writing and comparing.

test.describe('F20: cannot add more than the stock', () => {
  test('quantity above stock shows a readable error, nothing is added', async ({ page }) => {
    await fresh(page, 'f20');
    await addFromDetails(page, 'Hiking socks', 4); // stock is 3
    const message = page.getByText(/Only 3 of Hiking socks in stock/);
    await expect(message).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
    await expect(page.locator('body')).not.toContainText(/Exception|stack|500|INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR|\bat com\./);
    await shots(page, 'F20', 'error-quantity');
    // the refused dialog stays open so the customer can change the quantity: go back out of it
    await page.getByRole('dialog').getByRole('button', { name: /Back/ }).click();
    await openCart(page);
    await expect(page.getByTestId('cart-empty')).toBeVisible();
  });

    await addFromCard(page, 'Hiking socks');
    await addFromCard(page, 'Hiking socks'); // 2 + 1 + 1 = 4 > 3

    await shots(page, 'F20', 'error-cumulative

test.describe('F09: product image', () => {
  test('the seeded product view shows its uploaded image', async ({ page }) => {
    await openProducts(page);
    await openProductRow(page, 'Running shoe');
    // the generated binary field shows the stored file by name (it is an upload control, not a picture)
    await expect(page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Image/ })).toHaveValue(/\.png$/);
    await expect(page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Image/ })).not.toHaveValue('');
    await shots(page, 'F09', 'admin-product-view');
  });
});

async function removeTempProducts(page: Page) {
  await goToMenu(page, 'Products');
  await expect(tableHelper.getRows(page).first()).toBeVisible();
  while ((await page.getByRole('gridcell', { name: /^Temp tent/ }).count()) > 0) {
    await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, page.getByRole('gridcell', { name: /^Temp tent/ }).first(), /.*~get$/);
    await deleteOpenProduct(page);
  }
}

[4436] TOOLRESULT: async function setSetting(admin: Admin, label: RegExp, value: string) {
  await openFirstRow(admin.page, 'Shop settings');
  await typeNumber(admin.page, admin.page, label, value);
  await saveView(admin.page);
  await btn(admin.page, 'Back').click();
}

async function openByName(page: Page, menu: string, name: string) {
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: menu, exact: true }).first().click();
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, page.getByRole('gridcell', { name, exact: true }), /.*~get$/);
}

async function createCoupon(admin: Admin, c: { code: string; percent: number; from: Date; to: Date; limit: number; min: number }) {
  const a = admin.page;
  await a.getByRole('button', { name: 'Coupons', exact: true }).first().click();
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(a, a.getByRole('button', { name: /Create/ }).first(), /.*template$/);
  const d = a.getByRole('dialog');
  await expect(d).toBeVisible();
  await typeInto(a, d, /^Code/, c.code);
  await typeNumber(a, d, /^Percent/, String(c.percent));
  await typeDate(a, d, /^Valid from/, c.from);
  await typeDate(a, d, /^Valid until/, c.to);
  await typeNumber(a, d, /^Usage limit/, String(c.limit));
  await typeNumber(a, d, /^Minimum order value/, String(c.min));
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(a, d.getByRole('button', { name: /Create$/ }), /.*(list|~create)$/);
  await expect(a.getByRole('dialog')).toBeHidden();
  await expect(a.getByRole('gridcell', { name: c.code, exact: true })).toBeVisible();
}

[4469] ASSISTANT: ## Round 12 report: quick UI fixes and Level 6 second half (F45, F48–F54)

I committed everything locally as `659e439` and did not push. Both suites passed in one full run on the final build.

### Test results
- **Admin suite:** 35 passed.
- **Customer suite:** 54 passed, 1 skipped (the old empty-state test).
- **F54 in CI mode:** `CI=true npx playwright test -c playwright.ci.config.ts checkout-ci` passed headless with no retry used.
- **F53 integration tests:** 8 passed with `cd application/order-tests && mvn test`.
- **Earlier failures, now fixed:** a failure in a full run was fixed and I re-ran only that test. This applies to F36 coupons, F42 reviews, F48, S11 and F20. All of them passed in the final full run.
- **F10 variants (admin):** flaky. A cleanup error hid the real one; the cleanup no longer throws.

### The two quick UI fixes and the wishlist price
- **Breadcrumb:** The Notifications page now shows "Home". The bell navigated with `navigate`, which pushes the current page into the breadcrumb. The sidebar uses `clearNavigate`, and the bell now does too.
- **Header:** The bell and language switch now share one row with no stray background. The drawer stacked them and stretched the bell button across its width, which was the grey ellipse. One wrapper Box fixes it.
- **Wishlist price:** It now uses the shared Price component ("12,00 EUR" in Hungarian). No documented table route works, so I rebuilt the wishlist as a view page with a custom list, following the cart pattern. See issue 2.

### Built, per function
| ID | What was built |
|---|---|
| F45 | A "Change prices" row action on each category with a percent input. It runs in one transaction and rounds to 2 decimals. It refuses 0, −100 or less, +1000 or more, and a result of 0 or less. Only the list price changes. Test: +10% on a test category changes its two products (100.00 → 110.00, 25.50 → 28.05) and leaves an uncategorised product alone. |
| F48 | A Dashboard page showing orders today, revenue today and a best-sellers table with 5 rows per page. Cancelled orders are excluded. The test compares numbers before and after a new order, and after cancelling it. |
| F49 | Returns, a second state machine (REQUESTED → APPROVED or REJECTED), owned by the order line. Delivery starts a window set in Shop settings (14 days by default). Approval restores stock and the history shows the refund. The new pages are "Return an item", "My returns" and "Returns to review". |
| F50 | An interceptor writes one audit row per changed product field when an admin edits a product. The bulk price change writes its own rows. The "Audit log" page is read-only. |
| F51 | Not read from a settings row. The logo, title and primary colour stay in the model; the test checks them on five pages of each application. See issue 9. |
| F52 | A real migration, run twice, with no wipe (details below). |
| F53 | Order lifecycle integration tests. |
| F54 | A checkout test with a required address, run headless with the catalog's CI settings. |

- **F52 proof:** The 8 products keep name, SKU and price, and show the backfilled weight of 500. The settings row got a return window of 14. The 44 orders (later 88) survived both migrations. The Playwright test compares the table with a fixture taken from the database before the migration.
- **F52 second migration:** It added the stored `soldUnits` for F48, backfilled by an SQL join over the order lines.
- **F49 date rule:** The date rule is a model expression. The test sets the window to 0 days through the settings page and back to 14.
